The balance is the sum of that account's transaction amounts. The check runs against the database for every account a command would change, before that command writes anything.\n\n## Trigger\n\nInvoked by every command that changes transactions: `txn post`, `txn amend`, `txn delete`, `transfer`, and `load transactions`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `check_balances_after(changes)` takes a list of `(account, delta_cents)` pairs describing the proposed change. A delta is the signed cents a command would add to that account.\n- For each distinct affected account, it computes the current balance as the sum of the account's transaction amounts read from the database, then adds the summed deltas for that account.\n- If any resulting balance is below zero, it raises a rule-breach error that the calling command maps to exit `3`, and nothing is written.\n- If every resulting balance is zero or greater, it returns normally and the calling command proceeds to write.\n- An account with no transactions has a current balance of zero.\n- The check is read-only.\n- Several changes to one account in the same command are summed before the comparison, so a transfer that drives one account negative is rejected even if each leg alone would not be.\n\n## Access\n\n| Pattern | Owner | Access | Operation |\n|---------|-------|--------|-----------|\n| FEATURE-COUNT-003 | FEATURE-Count.md | read | `transactions.list` |\n\nThe balance check reads the transaction list through the same declared read operation used by Count, and it cites that pattern rather than adding one. Shared validators keep the rule identical across account add, rename, load, and post.\n\n## Trigger\n\nInvoked by every command story that accepts a code, name, description, or memo, before any write.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `validate_code(value)`: accepts 1 to 12 characters, each from `A-Z` or `0-9`. Lowercase, punctuation, whitespace, and any other character is rejected.\n- `validate_name(value)`: accepts 1 to 40 characters for names and descriptions. Used for account names, category descriptions, and any other 1-to-40 text field.\n- `validate_memo(value)`: accepts 0 to 60 characters. An empty string is valid and is stored as an empty memo.\n- All three reject any value containing a comma (`,`) or a newline (`\\n` or `\\r`).\n- A rejected value raises a validation error that the calling command maps to exit `3` with one diagnostic line on standard error and nothing on standard output.\n- Validators are pure: they read no database and write nothing.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC field-code-accepts-valid ===\nIntent: Codes of 1 to 12 characters from A-Z and 0-9 are accepted and returned unchanged.\nRequires: executable=python3; Money never passes through floating point at any layer.\n\n## Trigger\n\nInvoked by every command that accepts an amount: `txn post`, `txn amend`, `transfer`, and `load transactions`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `parse_amount_cents(text)` accepts input matching `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]`: an optional leading minus, one or more digits, and an optional decimal point followed by one or two digits.\n- The parsed value is non-zero. `0`, `0.00`, and `-0.00` are rejected.\n- Accepted input returns a Python `int` of cents, computed by integer arithmetic on the digit strings (for example `\"-12.5\"` returns `-1250`, `\"45\"` returns `4500`).\n- Any other input (a third decimal place, a `+` sign, a thousands separator, whitespace, exponent notation, a non-numeric string) is rejected.\n- A rejected amount raises a validation error that the calling command maps to exit `3`.\n- `format_cents(cents)` returns the decimal text with exactly two decimal places, a leading `-` when negative, and no thousands separator (for example `-1250` returns `-12.50`, `0` returns `0.00`, `500000` returns `5000.00`).\n- No function in this feature uses floating point.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC amount-parse-valid-forms ===\nIntent: Valid amount forms parse to the integer cents they denote.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.money import parse_amount_cents\n\nassert parse_amount_cents(\"45\") == 4500\nassert parse_amount_cents(\"45.5\") == 4550\nassert parse_amount_cents(\"45.50\") == 4550\nassert parse_amount_cents(\"-12.34\") == -1234\n=== END AC amount-parse-valid-forms ===\n\n=== AC amount-parse-rejects-zero ===\nIntent: Zero amounts, including a negative zero, are rejected.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.money import parse_amount_cents, ValidationError\n\nfor bad in (\"0\", \"0.00\", \"-0.00\"):\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        parse_amount_cents(bad)\n    except ValidationError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n=== END AC amount-parse-rejects-zero ===\n\n=== AC amount-parse-rejects-malformed ===\nIntent: Amounts with three decimals, a plus sign, a separator, whitespace, or non-numeric text are rejected.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.money import parse_amount_cents, ValidationError\n\nfor bad in (\"1.234\", \"+5\", \"1,000.00\", \" 5\", \"abc\", \"1e3\", \"\"):\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        parse_amount_cents(bad)\n    except ValidationError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n=== END AC amount-parse-rejects-malformed ===\n\n=== AC amount-format-two-decimals ===\nIntent: Stored cents format to text with exactly two decimals, a leading minus when negative, and no separator.\nRequires: executable=python3; `2026-02-29` is not.\n- Impossible days are rejected, including `2026-02-30`, `2026-04-31`, and month `00` or `13`.\n- Any other form (missing zero padding such as `2026-1-05`, a time component, a slash separator, whitespace) is rejected.\n- Returns the input unchanged when valid. A rejected date raises a validation error that the calling command maps to exit `3`.\n- The validator reads no database and writes nothing.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC date-accepts-real-days ===\nIntent: Ordinary dates and a leap day in a leap year are accepted and returned unchanged.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.dates import validate_date, ValidationError\n\nfor bad in (\"2026-02-30\", \"2026-02-29\", \"2026-04-31\", \"2026-00-10\", \"2026-13-01\"):\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        validate_date(bad)\n    except ValidationError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n=== END AC date-rejects-impossible-days ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-malformed-form ===\nIntent: Dates not in the exact YYYY-MM-DD form are rejected.\nRequires: executable=python3; The balance is the sum of that account's transaction amounts. The check runs against the database for every account a command would change, before that command writes anything.\n\n## Trigger\n\nInvoked by every command that changes transactions: `txn post`, `txn amend`, `txn delete`, `transfer`, and `load transactions`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `check_balances_after(changes)` takes a list of `(account, delta_cents)` pairs describing the proposed change. A delta is the signed cents a command would add to that account.\n- For each distinct affected account, it computes the current balance as the sum of the account's transaction amounts read from the database, then adds the summed deltas for that account.\n- If any resulting balance is below zero, it raises a rule-breach error that the calling command maps to exit `3`, and nothing is written.\n- If every resulting balance is zero or greater, it returns normally and the calling command proceeds to write.\n- An account with no transactions has a current balance of zero.\n- The check is read-only.\n- Several changes to one account in the same command are summed before the comparison, so a transfer that drives one account negative is rejected even if each leg alone would not be.\n\n## Access\n\n| Pattern | Owner | Access | Operation |\n|---------|-------|--------|-----------|\n| FEATURE-COUNT-003 | FEATURE-Count.md | read | `transactions.list` |\n\nThe balance check reads the transaction list through the same declared read operation used by Count, and it cites that pattern rather than adding one. Account-scoped reads are computed in the persistence layer's query, not by a new repository operation.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-COUNT-003` — read: list transactions - returns: `account`, `amount`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC balance-allows-zero-result ===\nIntent: A change that leaves an account at exactly zero is allowed.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\nfrom cli.balance import check_balances_after\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert code == 0\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"CASH1\", name=\"Till One\")\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"CASH1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=500, memo=\"\")\n    check_balances_after([(\"CASH1\", -500)])\n=== END AC balance-allows-zero-result ===\n\n=== AC balance-rejects-negative-result ===\nIntent: A change that would leave an account below zero raises a rule breach and leaves the table unchanged.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\nfrom cli.balance import check_balances_after, RuleBreach\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert code == 0\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"CASH1\", name=\"Till One\")\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"CASH1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=500, memo=\"\")\n    before = db.transactions.list()\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        check_balances_after([(\"CASH1\", -501)])\n    except RuleBreach:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    assert db.transactions.list() == before\n=== END AC balance-rejects-negative-result ===\n\n=== AC balance-sums-changes-per-account ===\nIntent: Several changes to one account are summed before the comparison, so a combined breach is rejected even when each leg alone is allowed.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\nfrom cli.balance import check_balances_after, RuleBreach\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\nassert code == 0\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"CASH1\", name=\"Till One\")\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"CASH1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=500, memo=\"\")\n    check_balances_after([(\"CASH1\", -300)])\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        check_balances_after([(\"CASH1\", -300), (\"CASH1\", -300)])\n    except RuleBreach:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n=== END AC balance-sums-changes-per-account ===\n\n=== AC balance-empty-account-is-zero ===\nIntent: An account with no transactions has a balance of zero, so any debit is rejected and any credit is allowed.\nRequires: executable=python3;
